[^3135]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Смиренную душу мою, посети Гди, въ грѣсѣхъ житїе все иждившую, яко блудницу прїими и мене, и спаси мя.' Biblical typology of the penitent sinful woman (harlot) who anointed Christ's feet with myrrh (Luke 7:37-38).
[^3136]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Преплавая пучину сегѡ житїя... Петровъ провѣщаю ти гласъ, спаси мя Христе'. Allusion to St. Peter sinking in the Sea of Galilee and crying 'Lord, save me' (Matthew 14:30).
[^3137]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Стѣна непобѣдимая намъ хрстїанѡмъ еси Бце Дво'. Classic Marian hymnographic epithet of the Theotokos as the unbreachable fortress and constant intercessor of the faithful.
[^3138]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Днесь Агглская воинства, въ память страстоносецъ прїидоша'. Tone 4 martyrikon chanted at Ode 6 of Matins on Lenten weekdays when the Menaion provides no festive kontakion.
[^3139]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Скорѡ снидемся в чертогъ хртовъ... съ причастницы суще мудрымъ дѣвамъ'. Parable of the Ten Virgins (Matthew 25:1-13), a dominant eschatological trope of Holy Week and Lenten weekday matins.
[^3140]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Множествомъ согрѣшенїй моихъ осуждаемъ есмь... согрѣшихъ Гди спаси мя'. The cry of compunction (умиление) addressed to Christ as Master of life and death.
[^3141]: Physical leaf coordinate: p861 (fol. 249r). Church Slavonic: 'Слово Ѻтчее, Хрта Бога нашего... изъ тебе воплотившася разумѣхомъ Бце Дво'. Text breaks at bottom of fol. 249r with catchword 'едина' and concludes on fol. 249v.
[^3142]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Conclusion of Tone 4 Tuesday sessional theotokion: 'едина блгсловенная. тѣмже непрестаннѡ, тебе воспѣвающе величаемъ'.
[^3143]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Church Slavonic: 'Кртомъ въѡружившеся страстотерпци твои... даютъ исцѣленїя вѣрою просящимъ'. Tuesday martyrikon at Ode 6; the Passion-bearers overcome demonic stratagems through the weapon of the Cross.
[^3144]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Church Slavonic: 'Искупилъ ны еси ѿ клятвы законныя, чтною ти кровїю'. Direct citation of Galatians 3:13 ('Christ hath redeemed us from the curse of the law, being made a curse for us').
[^3145]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Church Slavonic: 'На Кртѣ тя пригвоздиша Іудеи Спсе... простерлъ еси длани на немъ волею'. The voluntary extension of Christ's hands upon the Cross to gather the scattered nations.
[^3146]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Church Slavonic: 'Дво всенепорочная, Мти Хрта Бога, ѡружїе пройде твою пртую душу'. The prophecy of Simeon fulfilled at Golgotha (Luke 2:35).
[^3147]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Church Slavonic: 'Силою Кртною подвигшеся добрѣ... млтвами вашими сщеннїи Хви ѡружницы'. Wednesday martyrikon at Ode 6; the martyrs hailed as sacred armor-bearers of Christ.
[^3148]: Physical leaf coordinate: p862 (fol. 249v). Rubric marking the start of Thursday Sessional Hymns to the Apostles in Tone 4, whose text opens on fol. 250r with catchword 'Свѣ-'.
[^3149]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Свѣтильники въ конца Ѹчнки своя Хрте явилъ еси'. Thursday Tone 4 Apostolikon; the Apostles dispatched as spiritual luminaries to dispel the darkness of polytheism.
[^3150]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Вѣрѣ проповѣдники, учнки твоя Спсе мїрови показалъ еси'. The Apostles illumine the world with the radiant rays of the divine Word.
[^3151]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Яко всѣхъ тварїй лучши еси, и славити тя достойнѡ, недоумѣемъ Бце'. Inability of human language to praise the Mother of God, who transcends all creation.
[^3152]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Мужески раны претерпѣвше, достойнѡ воспрїясте цртвїя нетлѣнный вѣнецъ'. Thursday martyrikon at Ode 6; incorruptible crowns awarded for enduring bodily afflictions.
[^3153]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Скорѡ предвари даже непоработимся... да уразумѣютъ колико можетъ православныхъ вѣра'. Friday sessional hymn to the Cross, historically invoked in times of ecclesiastical and civic trial for the defense of the Orthodox commonwealth.
[^3154]: Physical leaf coordinate: p863 (fol. 250r). Church Slavonic: 'Язвенно ти влко копїемъ, Бжественнымъ ребромъ... ѡружїя ѡскудѣша невидимагѡ врага'. The piercing of Christ's side with the lance (John 19:34) disarms the adversary. The leaf concludes with catchword 'На кртѣ' for the stavrotheotokion on fol. 250v.
[^3155]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'На кртѣ тя вознесенна яко видѣ, Пречтая Мти твоя слове Бжїй'. Friday stavrotheotokion opening fol. 250v; maternal lament of the Virgin witnessing the Creator's death.
[^3156]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Cross-reference rubric directing the reader back to Monday of Tone 4 for the text of the martyrikon ('Днесь Агглская воинства... Зри въ пне: сегѡ гласу', see fol. 249r / p861).
[^3157]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'Судїи сѣдящу, и Агглѡмъ предстоящимъ. трубѣ гласящой, и пламени горящу'. Graphic eschatological depiction of the Dread Judgment based on Daniel 7:9-10 and Matthew 25:31-46.
[^3158]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'Вси побдимъ, и Хрта усрящимъ вси, съ множествомъ елеа'. Lenten exhortation to spiritual vigilance and good works in light of the closed bridal doors of the Parable of the Ten Virgins.
[^3159]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'Съ Агглы нбная, съ члвѣки земная... Радуйся двери нбсъ пространнѣйшая'. Theotokion celebrating the Mother of God as the portal wider than the heavens (*Platytera ton ouranon*).
[^3160]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'Сїяетъ днесь память страстотерпецъ, иматъ бо ѿ нбсъ заря'. Monday martyrikon at Ode 6 in Tone 5, uniting heaven and earth in commemoration of the holy martyrs.
[^3161]: Physical leaf coordinate: p864 (fol. 250v). Church Slavonic: 'Душе, здѣ сущая временная суть, тамошная же вѣчная'. Folio 250v breaks after 'тамошная же' with catchword 'вѣч-' and concludes on fol. 251r.
[^3162]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Conclusion of Tone 5 Tuesday penitential sessional hymn: 'вѣчная: зри судище, и на пртолѣ судїю, и трепещи ѿвѣта'.
[^3163]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'На ложи слежа грѣхѡвъ многи, ѡкрадаюся надеждою спсенїя моего'. The slumber of spiritual apathy and bodily sloth depicted as a snare depriving the soul of salvation.
[^3164]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'Странно дѣвы таинство, мїрови явися спсително. изъ нея бо родися безъ сѣмене'. Virginal conception without human seed (*sine semine*).
[^3165]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'Чудеса стыхъ Мчникъ... Царствїя скиптры утверди'. Civic-liturgical petition embedded in the martyrikon of Tone 5, praying for the stability of royal and sovereign authority through the martyrs' intercessions.
[^3166]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'Мѣсто лобное, Рай бысть: токмѡ бо водрузися древо кртное, абїе возрасти гроздъ животный'. Theological typological parallel between Golgotha (*Locus Calvariae*) and the Garden of Eden: the Tree of the Cross brings forth the true Vine and Cluster of Life.
[^3167]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'Распеншагося Спса, и избавителя нашего, волею яко вѣсть'. Voluntary nature of the Passion (*sponte passus*).
[^3168]: Physical leaf coordinate: p865 (fol. 251r). Church Slavonic: 'Вознесенна на древѣ яко видѣ, рождшая тя Спсе безъ болѣзни, рыдаше'. Marian lament at Golgotha contrasting the painless virginal birth with the sorrow of the Crucifixion.
[^3169]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Conclusion of Tone 5 Wednesday martyrikon from fol. 251r ('вожделѣвше. ѡставиша житейскую красоту... подаждь душамъ нашымъ умлтивенїе'). Evocation of Christ's question regarding the cup of martyrdom (Matthew 20:22).
[^3170]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Яко самовидцы слова и слугы, премудрыя Аплы похвали... и покланяющихся мощемъ ихъ'. Direct reference to Luke 1:2 (*autoptai kai hyperetai tou logou*) and explicit liturgical veneration of apostolic relics.
[^3171]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Аплы согласно восхвалимъ, яко проповѣдавшы всѣмъ православно Гдне ученїе, и ѿгнавшихъ мглу ересей'. Apostolic preaching praised as the foundation of Orthodox dogma against the darkness of heresy.
[^3172]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Ѡсужденъ судомъ совѣсти, и прежде суда судъ помышляя и трепещу ѡкаянный'. Theological theme of self-condemnation before the inner tribunal of conscience anticipating the Last Judgment.
[^3173]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Стыхъ мчнкъ исправленїя, нбныя силы преудивиша'. Astonishment of the incorporeal hosts that mortal men vanquished spiritual wickedness through the Cross.
[^3174]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Кртъ претерпѣлъ еси своею волею, и ѿ тля члвѣки свободилъ еси Спсе'. Friday sessional hymn to the Cross celebrating freedom from mortal corruption.
[^3175]: Physical leaf coordinate: p866 (fol. 251v). Church Slavonic: 'Волею щедре распятїе претерпѣлъ еси, и древняя клятвы снѣди ради'. Text carries over from bottom of fol. 251v to fol. 252r with catchword 'тѣмже'.
[^3176]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Conclusion of Tone 5 Friday sessional hymn: 'тѣмже Бжественная и чтная страданїя твоя Влко Хрте, поемъ и покланяемся'.
[^3177]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Кртомъ Сна твоегѡ Бгорадованная, идѡлская лесть вся упразднися'. Abolition of pagan idolatry through the Cross of Christ.
[^3178]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Rubric cross-referencing Friday of Tone 5 for the martyrikon text ('Сїяетъ днесь: Зри въ Пятокъ, сегѡжъ Гласу', see fol. 250v / p864).
[^3179]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Помышляю день страшный, и плачуся дѣянїй моихъ лукавыхъ... блгоутробне Ѻтче, Сне единородный, Дше стый помилуй мя'. Trinitarian penitential supplication in Tone 6 on Monday after the 1st Stichology.
[^3180]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Въ юдолѣ плачевнѣ, въ мѣстѣ еже положилъ еси'. Scriptural motif of the 'valley of weeping' (*in convalle lacrimarum*, Psalm 83:7 [LXX 83:6 Masoretic 84:6]) as the setting for the divine judgment.
[^3181]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Преславную Бжїю Мтрь, и стыхъ Агглъ стѣйшую, немолчно въспоимъ'. The Mother of God praised as holier than the angelic orders.
[^3182]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Свѣтъ праведнымъ всегда: Стїи бо тобою просвѣтившеся, сїяютъ присно яко свѣтила'. Scriptural incipit from Psalm 96:11 (LXX: 'Light hath dawned for the righteous').
[^3183]: Physical leaf coordinate: p867 (fol. 252r). Church Slavonic: 'Помилуй насъ Гди, помилуй насъ. всякаго бо ѿвѣта недоумѣюще, сїю ти млтву, яко влцѣ, грѣшнїи приносимъ'. The universal penitential troparion used at the beginning of the Third Hour, Little Compline, and Midnight Office across all Byzantine recensions. Text continues onto fol. 252v.
[^3184]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Гди помилуй насъ, на тя бо уповахомъ, непрогнѣвайся на ны зѣлѡ'. Continuation of the classic penitential troparion set; echoes Psalm 32:22 and Isaiah 26:8.
[^3185]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Млрдїя двери ѿверзи намъ, блгословенная Бце'. The beloved Marian penitential dismissal troparion (*Eusplagchnias anoixon hemin tas pylas*).
[^3186]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Страдалческїй подвигъ претерпѣвше Стїи, и почести побѣды ѿ тебе прїяша'. Tone 6 Tuesday martyrikon at Ode 6; crowns of incorruption granted to the triumphant martyrs.
[^3187]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Кртъ твой Гди ѡстися, тѣмъ бо бываютъ исцѣленїя болящимъ грѣхами'. Sessional hymn to the Cross on Wednesday in Tone 6; the Cross as source of healing for souls diseased with sin.
[^3188]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Токмѡ водрузися древо Христе, Крта твоегѡ, ѡснованїя подвигошася смерти Гди'. Shaking of Hades and death at the planting of the Cross upon the earth.
[^3189]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Бце Дво, моли Сна своего, волею пригвоздившагося на кртѣ'. Sessional stavrotheotokion in Tone 6 beseeching Christ's mercy through His voluntary Passion and Resurrection.
[^3190]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Church Slavonic: 'Страдалческая сопротивленїя, въ подвизѣхъ, мучителскїя раны на муницехъ'. Tone 6 Wednesday martyrikon at Ode 6 describing the contest of the martyrs before earthly magistrates.
[^3191]: Physical leaf coordinate: p868 (fol. 252v). Rubric marking Tone 6 Thursday Sessional Hymns to the Apostles, continuing onto fol. 253r with catchword 'Яко'.
[^3192]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Яко посредѣ Ѹчнкъ твоихъ пришелъ еси Спсе, миръ даяй имъ'. Direct allusion to Christ's post-Resurrection appearance giving peace unto the Disciples (John 20:19).
[^3193]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Ѹчнци твои Іисе, въ конца земля посылаеми, языки блгочестно, яко уловивше'. The apostolic commission to evangelize all nations (Matthew 28:19; Mark 16:15).
[^3194]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Престая Бце, въ время живота моего не ѡстави мене, и члвѣчѣй помощи не увѣри мене'. Supplication to the Virgin Mary, echoing Psalm 145:3 ('Put not your trust in princes, in a son of man, in whom there is no help').
[^3195]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Гди, стыхъ твоихъ память днесь показася, яко Рай иже въ Едемѣ'. Thursday martyrikon at Ode 6 comparing the commemoration of the martyrs to the Garden of Eden.
[^3196]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Днесь Прроческое сбыся слово, се бо покланяемся на мѣстѣ, идеже стоястѣ нозѣ твои Гди'. Explicit citation of Psalm 131:7 (LXX: 'We will worship at the place where His feet have stood').
[^3197]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Гди ѡсудиша тя Іудее на смерть... чермное море жезломъ прошедшїи... и ѿ камене медъ ссавшїи, желчь ти принесоша'. Typological contrast between the miracles of the Exodus (parting of the Red Sea, water from the rock) and the ingratitude of the Passion (crucifixion, gall).
[^3198]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Church Slavonic: 'Едина непорочная, агнице дво Бце... Сну мой незлобиве, пою всестрашное твое смотренїе'. Stavrotheotokion addressing Christ as the gentle and guileless Lamb.
[^3199]: Physical leaf coordinate: p869 (fol. 253r). Rubric cross-referencing Monday of Tone 6 for the Ode 6 martyrikon ('Свѣтъ праведнымъ: Зри выше въ поне: тогѡ гласу', see fol. 252r / p867).
[^3200]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Codicological note: In the 1640 Kyiv print edition, the opening rubric for Tone 7 on Monday ('Въ Понедѣльникъ. По а҃ Стїхологїи, Сѣдалны Покаянны. Гласъ, з҃.') was omitted in the body forme and represented only by the catchwords 'Въ' / 'Гла́-' at the bottom of fol. 253r (p869). Furthermore, lines 1-4 of the first troparion on fol. 253v suffered a typesetting misalignment where the initial words were printed in the left margin ('Имущи', 'слезами', 'свобо-', 'ди').
[^3201]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Имущи [душе] [у]врачевство покаянїя, принуди [слезами] дыханїе зовущи, врачу душъ и тѣлъ... причти [мя блу]дницу и разбойнику и мытарю'. Penitential invocation linking the three archetypal gospel penitents: the harlot (Luke 7:38), the good thief (Luke 23:42), and the publican (Luke 18:13).
[^3202]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Петрово ѿверженїе, слезами ѡмылъ, и мытарева согрѣшенїя, воздыханїе простилъ'. Repentance of St. Peter (Matthew 26:75) and the justification of the publican.
[^3203]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Превосходиши силы нбныя, яко храмъ была еси бжтвенный, блгословенная Бце'. Tone 7 Monday sessional theotokion honoring the Virgin as the divine temple surpassing the angelic orders.
[^3204]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Стїи молите ѡставленїе даровати согрѣшенїй нашихъ, и ѿ надежныхъ золъ избавитися намъ'. Tone 7 Monday martyrikon at Ode 6.
[^3205]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Мытарєву покаянїю непоревновахъ, и блудницѧ слезы не стяжахъ'. Tuesday Tone 7 sessional hymn of compunction lamenting the lack of true sorrow like that of the publican and harlot.
[^3206]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Блудница слезы, и Петровы прїимый, и мытаря ѡправдавый, и глубины воздохнувша'. Troparion addressed to Christ the Savior imploring compassion on the despairing soul.
[^3207]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Радуйся Бце, невмѣстимагѡ на нбсѣхъ, вмѣстившая въ утробѣ своей... проповѣданїе, еяже ради возсїя намъ Еммануилъ'. Marian salutations highlighting the Incarnation of Emmanuel (Isaiah 7:14) and the containment of the Uncontainable God.
[^3208]: Physical leaf coordinate: p870 (fol. 253v). Church Slavonic: 'Стїи твои Гди, на земли подвигшеся, идѡлскую лесть упраздниша'. Tuesday martyrikon at Ode 6 in Tone 7; text breaks at the bottom of fol. 253v with catchword 'любче' and concludes on fol. 254r (Cohort 88).

[^3209]: Codicological note on the typesetting defect and lacuna between f. 253v (p870) and f. 254r (p871). In the 1640 Kyiv edition, the foliation is strictly consecutive (`сн҃г` to `сн҃д`), but the text skips from the catchword of Tuesday's Tone 7 Martyrikon (`чл҃ко-`) directly into the middle of the first Sessional hymn to the Cross on Wednesday: `...и҆ Кѷпари́съ, покланѧ́ющыти сѧ, побѣ́ды Царе́мъ на́шымъ...`. In the complete liturgical witness of the Octoechos and Triodion, the hymn begins: *The pine, the cedar, and the cypress, as we worship Thee, granting victories unto our Tsars...* (Сосна̀ и҆ ке́дръ и҆ кѷпари́съ, покланѧ́ющыисѧ ти...); cf. Isaiah 60:13.

[^3244]: Codicological and liturgical rubric governing the Photagogika (Exapostilaria) of the Octoechos, appointed during the Holy Forty Days and other fasting seasons when the Triadica (*Тро́ичны*) are sung at Matins in place of *The Lord is God*.

[^3252]: Exaposteilarion of the Octoechos, Tone 8.
[^3253]: Major landmark publication division: Part V of the 1640 Kyiv Lenten Triodion, containing the unabridged hagiographical *Life of Our Venerable Mother Mary of Egypt* by St. Sophronius, Patriarch of Jerusalem (ca. 560–638). In the Kyivan Typikon tradition, this text is appointed to be read at Matins of Thursday of the Great Canon (the Fifth Week of Great Lent) in two sections, and is also appointed for her feast on April 1 and the Fifth Sunday of Great Lent. Physical leaf p877 (f. 257r) is adorned with an ornate headpiece woodcut depicting the Holy Mandylion (*Ѡ҆́бразъ Гдⷭнь на Оу҆брꙋ́сѣ*) flanked by angels.
[^3254]: St. Sophronius's prologue quoting the words of the Archangel Raphael in Tobit 12:7 (*It is good to keep close the secret of a king, but it is honorable to reveal the works of God*) and alluding to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25:14–30 (the slothful servant who buried his master's talent).
[^3255]: St. Sophronius's solemn defense of his truthful recording of the oral tradition transmitted by Abba Zosimas, warning against corrupting or bartering the word of God; cf. 2 Corinthians 2:17.
[^3256]: St. Sophronius's charitable prayer for those who doubt or hesitate to believe the supernatural wonders wrought by God in the saints due to awareness of human weakness.
[^3257]: Sophronius's transition to the historical narrative of Abba Zosimas of Palestine.
[^3258]: Sophronius cites the Wisdom of Solomon 7:27 (*And being but one, she can do all things; and remaining in herself, she makes all things new; and in all ages entering into holy souls, she makes them friends of God and prophets*).
[^3259]: Sophronius explicitly distinguishes Abba Zosimas of Palestine (the venerable monk of the Jordan wilderness) from the heretical Zosimas (a Monophysite or Origenist partisan condemned in 6th-century ecclesiastical controversies).
[^3260]: Scriptural quotation: Matthew 5:8 (*Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God*).
[^3261]: The subtle demonic thought (*logismos*) of spiritual self-sufficiency and vainglory that tempted Abba Zosimas after fifty-three years of solitary ascetical monastic struggle.
[^3262]: The angelic admonition rebuking Zosimas's self-satisfaction and directing him to the Jordan wilderness, quoting the divine call of Abraham in Genesis 12:1 (*Get thee out of thy country, and from thy kindred, and from thy father's house*).
[^3263]: The departure of Abba Zosimas from his original Palestinian coenobium to the monastery of St. John the Baptist by the river Jordan.
[^3264]: Dialogue between the Abbot (*И҆гꙋ́менъ*) of the Jordan monastery and Abba Zosimas upon his arrival.
[^3265]: Abba Zosimas's humble response explaining that he has come seeking spiritual profit and edification.
[^3266]: The Abbot's discourse on divine healing, quoting the description of the Good Shepherd in John 10:11, 14 (*I am the good shepherd, and know My sheep, and am known of Mine*).
[^3267]: The ascetic rule, unceasing prayer, psalmody, and manual labor of the monks of the Jordan wilderness monastery.
[^3268]: The ancient monastic custom of the Jordan monastery during Great Lent: the closing of the gates and complete seclusion from the outside world.
[^3269]: The ritual departure of the monks into the deep desert on the First Sunday of Great Lent (Sunday of Forgiveness / Cheesefare) after the Divine Liturgy, the mutual rite of forgiveness (*мета́нїе*), and the chanting of Psalm 26:1 (LXX) [Masoretic Ps. 27:1]: *The Lord is my light and my Savior, whom shall I fear? The Lord is the defender of my life, of whom shall I be afraid?*.

[^3270]: Slavonic: паѯіма́дїа (from Gk. παξιμάδιον, paximadion), twice-baked barley bread or dry biscuit; со́чиво ква́шено водо́ю, pulses or lentils steeped in water. In traditional monastic usage across the Palestinian lavras, the ascetics observed diverse individual measures of bodily austerity during the Great Fast.
[^3271]: The custom of complete solitude and mutual avoidance among the Jordan lavra brethren during Great Lent is documented in the Lavra of St. Gerasimus and the Coenobium of St. Euthymius, where the monks scattered throughout the desert until Palm Sunday.
[^3272]: Slavonic: въ Нл҃ю ꙗ҆́же є҆́стъ пре́жде Животворѧ́щагѡ Сп҃са Вскр҃сенїѧ... съ Вхїемъ. That is, Palm Sunday (Entry of the Lord into Jerusalem), known in the Ruthenian Triodion tradition as Willow/Branch Sunday (Цвѣтоно́снаѧ / Вхїе).
[^3273]: Cf. Galatians 1:10 and Matthew 6:1. St. Sophronius emphasizes the evangelical virtue of inner, hidden ascetical labor performed purely before God rather than for human praise.
[^3274]: The 1640 Kyiv text encloses the conjunction "[и҆̀]" in brackets, reflecting collation against the Greek original (καὶ Ζωσιμᾶς).
[^3275]: The phrase "[и҆ постѧ́щасѧ]" is enclosed in brackets in the 1640 Kyiv print, denoting a textual witness collated from the Greek manuscripts.
[^3276]: The monastic horologion prescribes prayer at the canonical third, sixth, and ninth hours. Elder Zosimas paused his trek through the desert at midday (the sixth hour) facing east.
[^3277]: Slavonic: тѣле́сныѧ ѡ҆бновле́нїємъ. The Greek text reads οὐ πλέον τοῦ τραχήλου καθικνούμενον ("reaching no lower than the neck"). The intense desert sun had blackened St. Mary's skin and bleached her hair white as wool (cf. Daniel 7:9, Revelation 1:14).
[^3278]: The word "[ви́дѣлъ]" is bracketed in the 1640 text, supplied by the Kyivan correctors from Greek witnesses (οὐ γὰρ ἑωράκει).
[^3279]: Elder Zosimas, now past fifty-three years of age and having journeyed twenty days into the scorching interior desert, is granted physical vigor to run after the fleeing ascetic.
[^3280]: Slavonic: ра́бе и҆́стиннагѡ Бг҃а. Zosimas addresses the fleeing ascetic with the masculine vocative "O servant of the true God," unaware as yet that the ascetic is a woman.
[^3281]: The dialogue across the dry ravine marks the first encounter between St. Zosimas and St. Mary of Egypt.
[^3282]: St. Mary reveals her identity as a woman and requests Zosimas's outer monastic mantle (рꙋ́бище) to cover her nudity before conversing.
[^3283]: Elder Zosimas recognizes the prophetic grace of the Holy Spirit in her knowing his name and priestly office without prior human communication.
[^3284]: St. Mary's humility and ascetic restraint are shown in her modest covering and her inquiry into the reason for Zosimas's journey.
[^3285]: Both ascetics remain prostrate on the desert ground, each yielding precedence to the other in humility, repeating the monastic request for a blessing: "Благослови" (Gk. Εὐλόγησον).
[^3286]: Elder Zosimas explicitly recognizes that inward sanctity and the gifts of the Holy Spirit surpass outward sacerdotal rank.
[^3287]: The mutual blessing concludes with the traditional doxological formula: "Blessed is God, Who wills the salvation of human souls" (cf. 1 Timothy 2:4).
[^3288]: St. Mary inquires concerning the state of the Christian world, the Roman Emperors, and the peace of the Church, having heard nothing of human affairs during decades of desert seclusion.
[^3289]: Elder Zosimas attributes the peace of the Church and Empire to the intercessory prayers of desert solitaries.
[^3290]: The conjunctions "[и҆̀]" are printed in brackets in the 1640 Kyiv text, reflecting the Greek manuscript witnesses (καὶ τὸ προσταχθὲν).
[^3291]: The miraculous levitation of St. Mary during prayer—standing about a cubit (approximately 18 inches / 45 cm) above the ground—is solemnly affirmed by St. Zosimas under oath, invoking God the Word as witness.
[^3292]: Slavonic: да не ка́кѡ ꙋ҆́бѡ дꙋ́хъ бꙋ́детъ, и҆ мл҃твꙋ притвара́етъ. Zosimas experiences the traditional monastic fear of demonic delusion (prelest / φαντασία), wondering if the apparition is an evil spirit mimicking prayer.
[^3293]: St. Mary reads Zosimas's inner thoughts and dispels his fear of delusion by confessing her sacramental reality as a baptized Christian formed of dust and ashes (cf. Genesis 18:27).
[^3294]: St. Mary signs herself with the sign of the Cross upon forehead, eyes, lips, and breast, invoking divine deliverance from the snares of the adversary.
[^3295]: Sirach (Ecclesiasticus) 20:30 (and 41:14): "Wisdom that is hid, and treasure that is hoarded up, what profit is in them both?"
[^3296]: Elder Zosimas argues that divine Providence purposefully led him twenty days into the desert to uncover St. Mary's hidden life for the edification of the Church.
[^3297]: St. Mary's profound humility: she considers herself an unworthy vessel of sin, and fears that her past life will shock the holy elder.
[^3298]: The phrase "[нꙋжда́щꙋ сѧ и҆]" is bracketed in the 1640 Kyivan text, indicating an editorial restoration from Greek copies (συνεχομένου καὶ δακρύοντος).
[^3299]: St. Mary was born in Egypt in the mid-5th century and ran away to the great Mediterranean metropolis of Alexandria at the age of twelve, where she fell into an unrestrained life of wantonness.
[^3300]: St. Mary confesses that her sinful lifestyle in Alexandria was driven not by avarice or poverty, but by an overpowering addiction to sensual passion, which she describes as wallowing in the mire.
[^3301]: The Universal Exaltation of the Precious and Life-Creating Cross, celebrated on September 14, commemorates the recovery of the True Cross by Emperor Heraclius and its solemn elevation in Jerusalem by Patriarch Zacharias (AD 629), as well as its original finding by St. Helena (AD 326).
[^3302]: St. Mary halts her confession in holy shame, fearing to distress Zosimas and scandalize the very air with the recounting of her former dissoluteness.
[^3303]: Elder Zosimas urges St. Mary to continue her narrative, recognizing its spiritual profit as a supreme example of divine mercy and repentance for future generations.
[^3304]: St. Mary abandons her distaff/spinning-wheel (прѧ́слица)—her only legitimate means of sustenance in Alexandria—and boards the pilgrim ship bound for Palestine.
[^3305]: Cf. Ezekiel 18:23 and 33:11, and 2 Peter 3:9. St. Mary marvels at God's longsuffering patience in not suffering the earth to swallow her alive as it did Dathan and Abiram (Numbers 16:31-33).
[^3306]: Upon arriving in Jerusalem before the feast, St. Mary continued her wanton life among both local inhabitants and foreign pilgrims.
[^3307]: The Basilica of the Holy Sepulchre (the Church of the Anastasis and the Martyrium) built by Emperor Constantine the Great, where the True Cross was displayed for universal veneration.
[^3308]: An invisible divine power repeatedly repels St. Mary from the threshold of the basilica, while allowing the entire crowd of pilgrims to enter freely.
[^3309]: The moment of compunction and awakening: standing in the corner of the atrium, St. Mary realizes that her unrepentant lifestyle is the sole barrier preventing her from beholding the Holy Cross.
[^3310]: St. Mary's prayer before the Icon of the Theotokos in the courtyard of the Church of the Holy Sepulchre marks the classical patristic archetype of taking the Mother of God as Surety (Порꙋ́чница, Gk. Ἐγγυητής) of repentance.
[^3311]: The phrase "[є҆го́же роди́ла є҆сѝ,]" is bracketed in the 1640 Kyivan text, repeating the Greek witness (ὃν ἐγέννησας). St. Mary makes a solemn vow to renounce the world completely immediately after venerating the Cross.
[^3312]: Having made her vow of lifelong repentance, St. Mary finds the invisible barrier dissolved, and enters into the sanctuary to venerate the Relic of the True Cross.
[^3313]: Slavonic: рꙋкописа́нїе (Gk. χειρόγραφον), a written bond or covenant. St. Mary refers to her prayer before the Icon of the Theotokos as a binding covenant of perpetual dedication.
[^3314]: St. Mary fulfills her vow and immediately receives divine guidance directing her toward the Jordan wilderness.
[^3315]: Slavonic: ца́ты (from Latin centenionalis / Greek κεντηνάριον or Old East Slavic cjata, small copper/silver coins). The unknown benefactor gave her three small coins, with which she bought three loaves of bread.
[^3316]: The famous Monastery of St. John the Baptist (Prodromos / Qasr al-Yahud) situated near the traditional site of Christ's Baptism on the western bank of the Jordan, where St. Mary received Holy Communion before crossing into the eastern desert.
[^3317]: Cf. Psalm 54:8-9 (LXX): "Lo, I have fled far off and have lodged in the wilderness; I waited for Him that saves me from faint-heartedness and tempest."
[^3318]: St. Mary's hesitation to recount her ascetical warfare reflects the patristic teaching that recalling past temptations can reawaken passion if not guarded by grace.
[^3319]: St. Mary's warfare against bodily appetites, memory, and demonic songs lasted seventeen years—the exact duration of her sinful life in Alexandria—demonstrating a spiritual symmetry in her purification.

[^3320]: Scriptural citation: Deut. 8:3 ("...man doth not live by bread only, but by every word that proceeds out of the mouth of the Lord doth man live"); Matt. 4:4; Luke 4:4.
[^3321]: Scriptural citation: Job 24:8 ("They are wet with the shower of the mountains, and embrace the rock for want of a shelter").
[^3322]: Elder Zosimas marvels at St. Mary's spontaneous quotation of the Holy Scriptures (the Pentateuch of Moses, the Prophets, and the Psalter), perceiving divine illumination in an unlettered desert dweller.
[^3323]: The Patristic tradition of infused divine wisdom (*theodidaktos*), wherein desert hesychasts, though illiterate according to worldly learning, were taught directly by the Holy Spirit.
[^3324]: Scriptural citation: Heb. 4:12 ("For the word of God is quick, and powerful, and sharper than any two-edged sword...").
[^3325]: Elder Zosimas responds with a full prostration (*metania*) and a liturgical doxology upon hearing St. Mary's confession of God's marvelous providence.
[^3326]: Scriptural citation: Job 5:9 ("Which does great things and unsearchable; marvelous things without number"); Job 9:10; Ps. 71:18 (LXX).
[^3327]: Scriptural citation: Ps. 9:11 (LXX 9:10: "And let them that know Thy Name hope in Thee: for Thou, Lord, hast not forsaken them that seek Thee").
[^3328]: St. Mary binds Zosimas under solemn adjuration not to reveal her life until after her repose, adhering to the foundational ascetic principle of hidden sanctity.
[^3329]: St. Mary prophetically foretells that Zosimas will be prevented from leaving the monastery during the upcoming Great Fast, and instructs him to bring the Holy Eucharist to the Jordan on Great and Holy Thursday.
[^3330]: The feast of the Mystical Supper on Great and Holy Thursday (Matt. 26:26–28; 1 Cor. 11:23–26), commemorating the institution of the Holy Eucharist.
[^3331]: Scriptural allusion: Acts 20:28 ("Take heed therefore unto yourselves, and to all the flock, over the which the Holy Ghost hath made you overseers..."). St. Mary exercises prophetic clairvoyance regarding the spiritual state of Abbot John's monastery.
[^3332]: Elder Zosimas experiences spiritual longing (*theios eros*) to see the venerable hermitess again, measuring the slow passage of the liturgical year against his desire.
[^3333]: Cheesefare Sunday (Forgiveness Sunday), whereon the Palestinian monastic rule prescribed that the brethren disperse into the deep Judean wilderness for the Forty Days after Vespers.
[^3334]: Great and Holy Thursday evening, the liturgical commemoration of the Last Supper whereat Christ distributed His Most Pure Body and Blood to the Apostles.
[^3335]: Sacerdotal administration of Holy Communion: Zosimas reserves the Holy Mysteries in a small pyx or chalice to commune the hermitess across the Jordan.
[^3336]: Monastic desert fare: *paximadia* (twice-baked bread/rusks), dried dates (*dattili*), and lentils steeped in water.
[^3337]: Elder Zosimas keeps vigilant watch on the Jordan bank, wrestling with anxious thoughts lest his personal unworthiness had hindered the saint's appearance.
[^3338]: Zosimas's prayer of repentance, fearing that failure to see the saint would serve as an indictment of his hidden sins.
[^3339]: The miraculous passage over the waters of the Jordan: St. Mary signs the surface of the river with the Precious Cross and walks upon the water. Compare Matt. 14:25–31.
[^3340]: St. Mary's profound reverence for the holy priesthood and the consecrated Eucharistic Gifts, forbidding the presbyter to prostrate before her while bearing the Mysteries.
[^3341]: The mutual greeting and reciprocal petition for blessing between the desert ascetic and the ordained hieromonk.
[^3342]: Elder Zosimas is overwhelmed by the miraculous vision and confesses the fulfillment of God's promise regarding the deification of the purified soul.
[^3343]: The Orthodox doctrine of deification (*theosis*): 2 Pet. 1:4 ("partakers of the divine nature"); St. Athanasius of Alexandria, *De Incarnatione*, 54.3.
[^3344]: The liturgical preparation for Holy Communion: the chanting of the Symbol of Faith (the Nicene-Constantinopolitan Creed) followed by the Lord's Prayer (Matt. 6:9–13).
[^3345]: The liturgical kiss of peace (*osculum pacis*). The 1640 Kyivan text provides an explicit bracketed gloss: `[сїе́ е́сть лобза́нїе:]` ("that is, the kiss").
[^3346]: St. Mary receives the Holy Mysteries of Christ for the first time in forty-seven years, weeping tears of thanksgiving.
[^3347]: The Canticle of the Righteous Symeon the God-receiver: Luke 2:29–30 (Nunc Dimittis).
[^3348]: St. Mary commands Zosimas to return one year later to the dry stream-bed where they first met, anticipating her upcoming departure to the Lord.
[^3349]: Zosimas begs to remain with the saint, offering her the simple provisions he had brought from the monastery.
[^3350]: St. Mary takes only three grains of steeped lentils upon her fingertips, declaring that the grace of the Holy Spirit suffices to preserve the soul undefiled.
[^3351]: St. Mary asks for prayers for the holy Church, the Christian Sovereigns, and herself, calling herself "wretched" in profound humility.
[^3352]: Zosimas returns in awe, sorrowing only that he had neglected to ask her name, but confident that he would learn it the following year.
[^3353]: In the subsequent year, after completing the customary Lenten fast, Zosimas returns to the dry watercourse and searches for the saint with tears, comparing his vigilance to that of a spiritual hunter.
[^3354]: Scriptural quotation: Heb. 11:38 ("Of whom the world was not worthy: they wandered in deserts, and in mountains, and in dens and caves of the earth").
[^3355]: Zosimas discovers the holy relics of St. Mary of Egypt lying peacefully upon the sand, her hands crossed over her breast and her face turned toward the East; he washes her feet with tears and chants the funeral psalms.
[^3356]: The miraculous inscription traced upon the sand beside the head of the reposed saint, revealing her name, date of death, and instructions for burial.
[^3357]: The sandy inscription records the name of St. Mary and establishes that she reposed on the very night of the Lord's Passion (Holy Thursday night / Good Friday), in the Egyptian month Pharmouthi (corresponding to April in the Roman/Julian calendar), immediately after receiving Holy Communion.
[^3358]: The miraculous translation of St. Mary across the desert: the distance that required twenty days of arduous journeying for Elder Zosimas was traversed by the saint in a single hour.
[^3359]: Zosimas laments his inability to dig a grave in the hard, parched desert soil without tools, finding only a small dry branch.
[^3360]: A great desert lion approaches the body of the saint, licking her feet in reverence and fawning upon the terrified elder.
[^3361]: Zosimas commands the lion to dig the grave with its claws, evoking the pre-fall harmony between man and creation described in the Patristic tradition (Gen. 2:19–20; St. Isaac the Syrian, *Ascetical Homilies*, 51).
[^3362]: Zosimas returns to the monastery and recounts the entire life of St. Mary of Egypt to Abbot John and the brethren, inaugurating her annual liturgical veneration.
[^3363]: Abbot John examines the monastery and enacts reforms to correct shortcomings among the brethren, confirming the prophetic word of St. Mary.
[^3364]: The hagiographical epilogue of St. Sophronius, Patriarch of Jerusalem (c. 560–638), testifying that the narrative was preserved through oral monastic tradition until he committed it to writing.
[^3365]: The classical Patristic modesty topos (*humilitas*) wherein the author acknowledges the possibility of other elevated accounts while affirming his fidelity to the historical truth.
[^3366]: Sophronius's pastoral benediction upon all readers and hearers who derive spiritual profit from the Life, and upon the ecclesiastical authority who commissioned its recording.
[^3367]: The Patristic harmony of ascetical action (*praxis*) and spiritual contemplation (*theoria*). The phrase `[ѿ вѣ́ка,]` ("from the ages") is preserved in brackets from the 1640 Kyivan imprint.
[^3368]: The solemn Trinitarian doxology concluding the Life of St. Mary of Egypt, formulated in strict accordance with the Father Paul Doxology Standard ("unto ages of ages").
[^3369]: Final colophonic seal: "AMEN" marking the conclusion of the unabridged Life of St. Mary of Egypt and the completion of the 1640 Kyiv-Pechersk Lenten Triodion under the archimandrisy of Metropolitan Petro Mohyla.
